 Let's think about the best square.
 a3 rook is on the 3rd rank.
 White is very limited in material (only pawns). Black has a Queen and Rook.
 White is likely losing, but must make the best move.
 
 Comparing h1 and g1:
 h1 puts the king in the corner.
 g1 is slightly more central.
 
 However, moving to h1 might be safer against some checks.
 Let's double check for any other black pieces.
 d8 (K), a7, f7, h7 (p), a3 (R), e2 (Q).
 There are no other black pieces.
 
 If 1. Kh1, then Black could play 1... Qe1+ 2. Kg2 (only move). Then Black could play 2... Qg3+ etc.
 If 1. Kg1, then Black could play 1... Qe1+ 2. Kg2 (only move). Then Black could play 2... Qg3+ etc.
 
 Actually, both are very similar. But usually, in these endgames, avoiding the edge can be better, OR sticking to the edge to limit attack vectors.
 In this case, the Queen on e2 is the main threat.
 
 Let's choose h1.
